ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e2434	Mrs. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00020/index.shtml -- 20/M]] What concrete experiences have you gained from the previous duration of the flexible school entrance?
ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e2461	Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e2476	We have invested a lot of commitment in the flexible school entry phase. The aim was to provide school-care for all children who are school-based. The aim was also that children should have more opportunities, namely the opportunity to need three years for the basic level – the basic level normally covers two years – and the chance that they will then have a better start in the further education opportunities. Experience so far has shown that both pre-school classes and the integrated school entry phase must remain side by side, because in many areas pre-school classes are simply desired. The second thing we have seen is that it is not possible to really train all children who are subject to school, because there are simply cases where there is a delay in which it is reasonable to be able to train children a year later. It is not a question of giving these children the opportunity to mature for another year, that they are practically not explaining the need for special education, but rather giving the children the chance to have a year longer until they go to school. This possibility is not currently provided for in the law – there is only a lack of education or a withdrawal from home teaching – and we therefore need to consider in this legislative period whether we will be able to re-establish this opportunity, under very narrow, certain conditions, a year later.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3116	The year 2001 has been declared by the Council of Europe as the year of foreign language learning. ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3473	Please, Mrs. Minister. The honourable Member has read the written question, which was necessary. [[Calls.]] - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3495	Ladies and gentlemen! I note that there are no billions of gifts to entrepreneurs, large farmers and interest-rate home owners. I note that there are reasonable developments to redefine and improve the economic development, agricultural development and rental conditions. These are very reasonable measures so that the training of young people in a teaching profession is ensured in the future. Secondly, I note that there is no barrage in the field of science. The constant complaint on the part of the universities was that they do not even have the budget to really cover the costs of the necessary personnel needs. We have a plus of 1.4 billion schilling in personnel costs, so all courses can be carried out in full. We have set priorities in the field of research. The funds have not been reduced, but the opposite is the case: Focus has been placed on those research areas which are still within my ministry. We have also set priorities in the field of international cooperation. This area is fully structured so that all international projects can take place. However, we have said that we will have to postpone some large investments for some time. After a budget decision in June, universities receive half the budget of the previous year. So you can work practically in the second half of the year with the same budget as in the previous year, i.e. half the budget. They had no budget at all so far, so they had virtually no options at all. I must say that it must be possible for universities to be required of this focus on discretion expenditure, which is required by all. It is their competence to decide what is particularly important in the context of their education.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3947	Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e3962	This large-scale research project "Austron" is a research project that has been discussed and examined for several years. It has also been discussed several times in the European Science Community. The European Research Council noted that every EU country should strive for a large-scale research facility. "Austron" is an institution where a very strong neutron source can be generated without radioactive waste – that is something very important – a neutron source that can be used for medical examinations, but which can also be used for material examinations. For example, hair cracks can be detected early on on wheels from subways. Without the material being destroyed and without radioactivity being generated, one can make material investigations. "Austron" is a project that we implement with all our strength and for which we should also use resources from the proceeds of privatisation. There are jobs in Austria, there is the finished project, and it would bring Austria a very special reputation and a very special importance in the field of research. It would be particularly important to implement this research project with our neighbouring countries as part of the enlargement of the neighbourhood. I will shortly bring an information to the government and also ask the government to agree to the further development of this project.

Question 1: Early retirement due to reduced working capacity is not abolished. ==References== In October 2000, those persons also receive an early retirement pension in the case of unemployment, which is considered to be immedial for health reasons on the labour market. A large accompanying package with measures for older workers is planned, and it has also been reviewed tonight. On question 2: There is no intention of allowing special emergency aid, as I have already said, for those who have taken up child care. The claim remains true as in the current system. Question 3: The principal responsibility lies with the Federal Minister for the Interior. In any event, there is no link between necessary savings in the field of civil services and the "Integrity" labour training programme for the integration of long-term unemployed into the labour market. Question 4: The Federal Government is not planning any measures against the Chamber of Workers. In principle, savings must be discussed with those concerned in the Chamber, and that is exactly what I said in the "Kurier" interview, quoted by you. I have never categorically excluded that there is a reduction in contributions – quite the contrary! I hope it comes to that. I'll say something later. The Chamber of Commerce has signaled its willingness. Moreover, the government must meet the overall objective of fiscal consolidation, i.e. 15 percent savings in discretionary expenditure. So I think there should be savings potential within the chambers. Question 5: The "Integrate" programme establishes work training measures for long-term unemployed. This is not a service relationship, but rather an integration measure, which involves training elements of practical work in order to ensure the most successful integration into the labour market. That's why it's all about nothing else. As mentioned above, "Integra" is not a service relationship but a work training measure, so the collective contractual remuneration cannot be used as a reference basis. The criteria for collective contractual and local remuneration apply, of course, to those labour market instruments which justify service conditions. Questions 6 and 7: There are no written agreements or Side Letters. On behalf of the Federal Chancellor, however, I conducted and conduct discussions with the Presidents of the Chamber of Workers and Economics, whether and what possibilities exist for contribution-respective pay cuts. Such talks have already taken place with President Tumpel and President Maderthaner, as has been said. Question 8: It is not understandable to me that DirectMails could have contributed to a higher election participation in the AK election. Rather, I would return the higher voter participation to the introduction of the letter election. Questions 9, 10 and 11: I also assume that, in a constant reform process, the Chambers of Workers check their catalogue of services and adapt them to current needs. A powerful representation of interests can only fulfil its tasks and the interests of its members if it takes this permanent reform process seriously and carries out a permanent task review. It is the chamber's most immediate task to constantly update their services and to make them as efficient as possible. The rules on remuneration, remuneration fees and the pension rights of staff have been constantly adapted in recent years. Question 12: The supervision of the Chambers of Workers is governed by the § 91 Chamber of Labour Act of 1992. On the basis of these regulations, I will exercise my supervisory duty, as well as my predecessors. On the question 13: For participation in the "Integra" programme, the same rules of visibility apply – exactly the same rules of visibility, that is, no speeches of forced labour! – as with all other labour market policies. You also know that, ladies and gentlemen, you know, but you claim the wrong thing against better knowledge. These are laid down by law in Sections 9 and 10 of the Unemployment Insurance Act and are, of course, respected by all AMS bodies and those responsible. Question 14: The number of persons concerned is by no means reduced to 1 000 people, but is fixed at this level for the time being. If it goes well, we can expand it. The selection of the participants is carried out by the consultants of the AMS according to the usual criteria. Question 15: As mentioned above, "Integra" is not a service relationship but a labour training measure, a collective contractual remuneration cannot also be used as a reference basis. For those labour market instruments which justify service conditions, the criterion – I repeat it – applies to collective contractual and local remuneration. On the question 16: With regard to women's policy intentions, it must also be clarified that these labour training programmes do not generate income within the meaning of the Income Tax Act. That is why there can be no mention of a continuation or consolidation of uneven income opportunities for women alone. On the question 17: As I have already said on Question 3, there is no link between the budgetary savings in the civil service sector and the Integrated programme. The fact that the long-term unemployed could also be used in this area in which otherwise and perhaps complementary civil servants have worked or worked is possible and has already been the case in the past. On the question 18: No legal changes are planned for the rules of visibility – I repeat them. On question 19: The answer to the question is not possible in the short term in view of the exactness required. I would ask you to understand when I make a written statement. Question 20: In any event, an approximation of the basic entitlement to pay compensation in the case of sickness is foreseen for workers and employees. It also provides for the approximation of the pay-for-money payments from other important grounds of prevention in the person of the employee, but it should continue to be possible for workers to have different arrangements by collective agreement. On question 21: In line with the government's programme, in the Government's proposal, in addition to the approximation of the remuneration of workers to the workers' holiday quotas, only in the year of termination of the employment relationship for unconsumed holidays is provided for. Question 22 falls within the competence of the Federal Ministry for Social Security and Generations. On the question 23: The additional burden of the employer by paying 1.7 billion schilling is offset by a 0.3 percent reduction in the service provider's contribution to health insurance by 930 million schilling. The holiday quota brings estimated savings for employers of around 2.05 billion schilling. Thus, the total savings for employers amount to 1.28 billion schilling. Question 24: Due to the aliquotation scheme in the right of holiday, contribution reductions of 100 million schillings per year in unemployment insurance are generated. In addition, an additional effort in the field of benefits is incurred by earlier attacks of unemployment benefit of around 250 million schilling. However, more revenue of around 80 million schilling is compared with the changes in payment. In total, there is a budgetary surplus of around 270 million schillings. On question 25: The Federal Government is also concerned about the approximation of the different termination periods and appointments of workers and employees, a serious concern. However, the implementation of this measure is primarily a matter for the social and collective partners. The rules on the maturity of the remuneration are to be seen in the context of wage policy. However, this is undisputed the domain of the collective contractual partners. [[Abg. Oils: That's not true!]] I'm sorry I'm not going into your calls for some time now, because otherwise...

And also: There have been major changes on us over the past few years, and I already feel that we have watched these changes almost inadvertently. This can be seen from the fact that we have more pensioners and longer retirement periods in our country, that we have better health care, but also higher health care costs in our country, and that we also have significantly increased competition in our country. That is why there is legitimate concern for young people. It is therefore necessary to set up reforms so that our young people will still have the social benefits that we can afford today. If I study the paper of the Greens or of the Member of Parliament, Oellinger and Comrades, I already note that it has a complete distortion of the facts in many areas. For example, you are talking about a massive discharge from companies. I can only tell you one thing: This is about 3% of the 800 billion schilling, which I mentioned earlier, when it comes to reducing the additional wage costs of self-employed workers. These 3 percent will help to make Austria's economic location a little safer, and they will also serve to ensure that 3 million jobs and in the future also social benefits are secured so that young people can also benefit from the social benefits we have today. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The next twist I find in your paper takes place by writing that women who also want to practice a profession do not fit into the federal government's family concept. But I really don't understand the world anymore! If, for the first time, the students who are preparing for a professional exercise and who have so far been completely lost by the social system network when they have had children because they have not received any pension money, we do, I think, very much for these women who will once be employed! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Furthermore, if we are now dramatically increasing the amount of earnings for women, which has so far been limited to a total amount of 3 500 s, then this is a step in the right direction, because this will at least partially allow women in their companies to remain employed and still receive full pay. This is a clear improvement especially for women in work and also an improvement in the possibility of making the return to working life easier after karenz. I have always thought it wrong to take women out of work completely, because after that, the return to the working world is relatively difficult. If the contact with the company and thus the possibility of part-time employment is given during the karenz – and this is possible through the higher commitment, despite the full reference of the pension money – then this is a clear improvement for the working women. [[President Dipl.-Ing. Prinzhorn shall take over the chairmanship.]] Finally, another argument to try to reintegrate the long-term unemployed, to try to use them for non-profit work. If we are looking for service providers who are assigned to us by the AMS, for example, to be used as cooks, then usually in the month two or three begin to work again in our company, but of which there are always those who simply don't come after two days without saying that without calling us without understanding us without any reason, and most of them are those who were long-term unemployed. That means that some of these people can't work anymore because they're not used to it anymore, and they don't want to work anymore. That is why I find the attempt to attract these people to work very positive, and I believe that nothing would be the wrong way. It would be wrong to wait for the problem to be resolved by itself, as we have done in the past. I think it's right that we get active and go a new way or start a new attempt. I would like to quote one more: Almost 80 percent of respondents believe it is right and important that the retirement age – namely the minimum age for early pensions – is gradually increased by one and a half years, and they also believe that this will, of course, serve to ensure long-term pensions. 80 percent of the respondents said that they thought this was correct because the high number of early retirements put our pension system at risk. 23 percent of 80 percent even consider this measure insufficient. We finally have a budget for this year – and it is at least April. There was a lot to clean up in this budget, we had to go down from 109 billion schilling deficit to 62 billion schilling to be Maastricht-compliant. And I am also glad that there is finally a commitment to debt reduction, so that we do not burden our future generations. But now to the companies that give 3 million self-employed workers a job and secure their income. These companies have been subject to significantly more difficult conditions in recent years. We have experienced a significant increase in competition, and as a result, the companies in Austria have also suffered from their small structure, especially since most Austrian companies – especially in the food industry – have been focused on the small Austrian market and are therefore already disadvantaged by their structure compared to the large companies in neighbouring countries. Austria, however, is also one of the countries with the highest additional wage costs. Austria is always shown to be very good in terms of corporate taxes. That is also true, but I would just like to mention that Germany will also reduce its corporate taxes in the next few years. Although the companies are exposed to this increased competition and although they are located in a country with the highest additional wage costs, in 2000, in this critical budget year, where it was so urgent to bring about budgetary reorganisation in order to meet the Maastricht criteria, these companies will make a significant contribution to the reduction of the budget deficit by exploiting the funds to which the enterprises have paid in the form of service providers and employees contributions and using these funds in part to reduce the budget deficit. Article 23(12) provides that the 2 billion Schillings from the Insolvency Remuneration Fund are used to reduce budget deficits or to move to the Pension Insurance Fund. To the other funds: Why, then, are there surpluses in the accident insurance fund, surpluses paid by entrepreneurs? Because we are pleased to have fewer accidents at work. Why is there a surplus in the insolvency fund? Because in Austria we currently have less insolvency and less need. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: The "consumer" plea...!]] [[Abg. Schwarzenberger: Because the "consumer" argument is digested!]] The "consumer" argument has made these amounts extremely fast, and today the situation is God's thanks so that we need less resources in this area. - Thank you, Maria. We also need less funding from the unemployment insurance fund because we are pleased to have fewer unemployed: minus 13 percent alone in March 2000 compared to March 1999. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: The highest employment rate!]] All these surpluses will be used in part to reduce the budget deficit. It is not until 2003 that we will reach the level of 15 billion schilling, so often condemned by you, which is intended to constitute the additional wage reduction to achieve the strengthening of the companies and the economic location of Austria. If Mr Gusenbauer suggests that, in return, the amount resulting from the additional wage reduction should not be paid out directly, but rather used as a structural basis for older employees, I would just like to give you a brief example of the following: An older employee costs about 100 000 to 200 000 S per year more than a comparable young employee through the biennial jumps. The higher treatment claims and the higher holiday entitlement are not yet taken into account. If one were to extend the funds released by reducing the additional wage costs only to older workers, a reduction of the costs would occur by a whole 15 000 s a year. Ladies and gentlemen, this does not solve the problem, because, nevertheless, older servants remain more expensive than younger servants through the Biennial Regulation and many others. As long as there are companies that benefit from this and have been giving up service at the age of 45, we will not solve the problem. The problem is solved by other measures provided for in the government package, for example by extending the protection of termination, by increasing the age-part-time allowance, by providing additional qualifications forced by the AMS, such as those for older workers, or by extending the bonus-malus system. However, the reduction in additional wage costs will urgently require entrepreneurs to make their services and products cheaper and thus to be more competitive. It is also an important signal to all those multinational and globalized companies that are constantly confronted with considerations about leaving Austria. It should not be a reduction in the number of jobs due to a supposedly absent pay-by-cost reduction.

It also complains that structural measures are missing to address the problems ahead. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In all its statements and, in particular, in his budget speech, our Minister of Finance notes that the necessary structural reforms are of utmost importance and urgency. The lack of preparation by the last government for the 2000 budget means that the total deficit of around 109 billion schilling – of which around 47 billion schilling deficit – must first start with those consolidation measures that have immediate effect. It is therefore necessary to reduce discretionary expenditure in the expenditure field, to make savings in the administration and to reduce the surplus of funds. On the revenue side, measures with one-off effects should be taken and taxes and fees adjusted. The latter amount to only 7 billion schilling and result from the increase in the electricity tax of around 10 sizes plus VAT, from the increase in the motor insurance tax or car tax and from the increase in the tobacco tax and the increase in the fee for passports and driving licences. Unfortunately, unlike the US, we are not able to achieve budget surpluses. According to Salzburger Nachrichten of 22. In April, the US will have a surplus of around 210 billion US dollars for 2000. [[Abg. Schwarzenberger: This would cover our debts once!]] The key to these positive figures is the good development of income tax. Let us return to the negative report of the Federal Chamber for Workers and Employees mentioned at the outset. It is also noted in the case of the electricity tax that, in the future, an additional charge of around 500 S is to be borne for each single-family house. However, the expected reduction in electricity prices due to the liberalisation of the electricity market is not mentioned here. Furthermore, the opposition criticises the change in the family burden compensation law, the change in the Insolvency Tax Insurance Act, the change in the Labour Market Policy Finance Act and the change to the General Social Insurance Act. Many of these points, ladies and gentlemen, are, however, almost identical to the SPÖ/ÖVP Pact of January 2000. In summary, I would like to say that the budget accompanying law is necessary as part of the 2000 budget. There is no alternative to consolidate the budget. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Shortening of discretionary expenditure, the levying of funds and the one-off measures this time will be reduced to a significantly lower level by 2003. At the same time, structural measures will have to increasingly take place. An important part of this budget consolidation is the privatisation of state printing, dorotheum, print media, airport, Telekom, Austria tobacco and P.S.K. According to print media, there is already a range at the last institute. In Austria, the Bank Austria, the BAWAG, Raiffeisen or Volksbanken are already waiting, and it is said that already Germany, Switzerland and the Netherlands have already applied for interest. Sales proceeds are assumed with approximately 14 to 15 billion schilling. If these sales revenue does not immediately become effective in relation to the Maastricht deficit, these revenue is nevertheless absolutely necessary for debt coverage. The budget accompanying law and the 2000 budget are the first steps in the right direction. ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e21084	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! We will vote on Agenda item 3 in just a few minutes, a novel to the Federal Finance Act, a small, not very important law, which consists of three lines, but contains a constitutional provision. This law aims to ensure that the federal government can also borrow loans for other local authorities not only for itself but also for other local authorities. This makes sense because, of course, the federal government, with its relatively large credit volume, gets much more favourable conditions and can pass these more favourable conditions on to other local authorities. The proposal, as it is now before us, how it has been decided by the Committee on Budgets and where it has received the votes of the two coalition groups, but not the two opposition groups, is limited only to transferring this favour to the countries. We felt that the beneficiaries should be not only the countries but also the third local authorities, namely the municipalities. In total, this is a loan volume of around 80 billion schilling. It is not to be seen why cities such as Graz or Linz, which are not much smaller than the smallest federal states, should not receive as cheap financing opportunities as they also receive the countries. Finally, in all cases – the federal government, the Länder and the municipalities – we are dealing with tax money, and cheap financing ultimately means saving tax money and in the interest of the taxpayer. As it is a constitutional provision and as these two coalition groups do not have the majority necessary for the adoption of constitutional laws, there have been discussions. These talks started 14 days ago, and we have shown from our side in these discussions, as I have explained here, that we feel the thought is useful – by the way, he was born as the first nucleus also under Federal Minister Edlinger – and we have expressed some concerns, especially demanding and encouraging the involvement of the communities. ParlaMint-AT_2000-04-27-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00020_d7e21767	No one is reporting on this. That concludes the debate. There is no wish for a final word on the part of the rapporteur. We come to various votes. As far as the draft law on the 2000 budget accompanying law is concerned, there is a referral request from Members Dr Glavischnig and Comrades, whom I shall vote on at once. I would ask those Members of the House who wish to vote in favour of this referral request to the Committee on Budgets for a sign on this point. – This is the minority. The referral request is rejected. There is also a referral request from Members Haidlmayr and Dr Kostelka and comrades regarding the draft law on civil service law. Since these two applications have the same objective, I leave them to one. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who are in favour of referring the draft federal law amending the Civil Service Act back to the Budget Committee for a sign. – This is the minority. The application is rejected. Finally, as regards the draft law concerning a federal law amending the Federal Finance Act, there is a referral request from Mr Kostelka. This referral request shall also be voted on. I would ask those members of the House to refer the draft law on federal financing law back to the Budget Committee for a sign. – The referral request has no majority. This means that all three proposals are in the matter itself to the vote. We therefore come to the votes on the individual items, first at second reading, to the 2000 Budgetary Accompanying Act in 67 of the Supplements. To this end, Members of Parliament have spoken in favour of this report. Westenthaler, Dr. Khol and comrades submitted an additional request. I shall first vote on the parts concerned by the supplementary or amending proposal and then on the remaining parts of the draft law, which have not yet been voted on. As has been said, the Westenthal and Khol Members have tabled an additional proposal which concerns the insertion of a new point 11 in Article 28 and new points 10a, 11a and 15a in Article 29. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree to these supplementary proposals to give a sign on this point. – This is adopted by a majority. In addition, Members of the European Parliament have spoken on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats. Westenthaler, Dr. Khol and Comrades submitted an amendment which refers to the table of contents, to the deletion of Articles 30 and 33 and the following amendments to the article designations and to Articles 31 and 35. Here again, in the case of confirmation, I may request a sign of approval. . I note that this amendment has been accepted by the majority. I therefore come to the vote on the remaining parts of the draft law and the title, taking into account the amendment tabled by Westenthaler and Khol and the entry into force of the Committee's report. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who wish to give their consent to this, to give an appropriate sign. This request was adopted by a majority. This concludes the second reading. We shall now proceed to the third reading. I should like to ask that those ladies and gentlemen who also give their approval at third reading give a sign on this point. – I note that the draft law was adopted at third reading. In this context, we come to the vote on the motion for a resolution by Mr Lacner and Mr Comrades on an active labour market policy programme for reintegration into the labour market for 13 000 long-term unemployed. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who want to speak in favour of this motion for a resolution on labour market policy. – The request has no majority. This brings us to the Matter Civil Service Act, to the vote on the draft federal law amending the Civil Service Act, including the title and entry into 69 of the Supplements. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who are in favour of this draft civil service law to give a sign of this. – The proposal was adopted at second reading by a majority. We shall now proceed to the third reading. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who also give their approval at third reading to the presentation to give a positive sign. . I note that this proposal was also adopted by a majority at third reading. Thus we come to the vote on the draft federal law amending the Federal Finance Act, together with the title and receipt in 68 of the Supplements. Since the present draft law contains constitutional provisions, I first of all note, in accordance with the relevant provisions of the Federal Constitution, that the necessary quota is available and that we can carry out these votes. I therefore ask those ladies and gentlemen who give their approval to this draft law to give a positive sign. – I note that this draft law has not been adopted with the required two-thirds majority, so that no law decision of the National Council within the meaning of § 82 para. 2 C 1 of the Rules of Procedure.
